Single-purpose: which\n  side(s) of an existing dual workspace.\n- **Topology** moves to a boolean `--por` flag at creation\n  time (init, clone). The `code,bot|por` grammar collapses\n  to \"do you want a `.claude` companion or not.\"\n\nAfter this cycle:\n\n- `Scope::Single(PathBuf)` — gone. `-R <path>` is the\n  path-only entry point; the `Scope` runtime type holds\n  only roles.\n- `parse_scope_roles` — gone. Its job (reject path forms in\n  the roles slot) is moot once paths aren't a roles form.\n- `options_flags/scope.rs` (`ScopeKind`, `ScopeOption`,\n  `parse_scope_kind`, `ScopeParser`) — replaced by a\n  `PorFlag` leaf in `options_flags/`.\n\nThe deeper symmetric-`.vc-config.toml` refactor (todo #11)\nis independent and lands in `0.54.0`; keeping it separate\nkeeps each cycle reviewable.\n\n### Todo items the cycle absorbs\n\nDeletes:\n\n- #5 (was #4): \"Drop `-R/--repo` from `CommonArgs`\" —\n  premised on folding `<path>` into `-s`, rejected here.\n- #6 (was #5): \"`-s <path>` workspace-root override\" —\n  same premise, same fate.\n- #14 (was #13): \"init dual|por arg split\" — settled\n  with `--por` flag (a third shape neither half of the\n  entry considered).\n\nRewrites / light touch:\n\n- #2 (was #1): drop `scope` from the single-field\n  leaves sweep — `ScopeOption` is being deleted.\n- #13 (was #12): `--scope` sweep continuation —\n  `Single(_)` dogfood half drops out; only the\n  `finalize --scope` replacing `--repo` half survives.\n- #19 (was #18), #20 (was #19): push/clone\n  `--scope=...|<path>` flag designs — `<path>` form\n  drops out.\n- #21 (was #20): validate-desc/fix-desc `--scope` —\n  `Single(_)`-errors-here parenthetical moot.\n\nA new entry #1 (`vc-x1 fix-todo` / `validate-todo`)\ncaptures the future Rust replacement for the interim\n`notes/fix-todo.py`.\n\n### Notes-side prep done at -0\n\nThe cycle's todo edits needed clean ordinal references, so\nthe opener also lands a notes-convention change:\n\n- `## Todo` / `## Bugs` entries now use manual numbering\n  (`1.` `2.` …) rather than lazy `1.` everywhere. The raw\n  source is readable without rendering, at the cost of\n  diff churn on reorder. See the convention prose in\n  todo.md.\n- `notes/fix-todo.py` — interim Python script that\n  renumbers and shifts continuation indent on a delete /\n  reorder. Slated for Rust replacement (todo #1).\n\n### Ladder\n\n- 0.53.0-0 plan + version bump + notes convention +\n  fix-todo script + todo edits (current)\n- 0.53.0-1 collapse `src/scope.rs` to roles-only —\n  drop `Scope::Single`, `parse_scope_roles`; simplify\n  parser + helpers\n- 0.53.0-2 replace `ScopeKind`/`ScopeOption` with\n  `--por` flag — delete `options_flags/scope.rs`;\n  migrate init / clone / test_helpers\n- 0.53.0-3 docs/script sweep — README, ARCHITECTURE,\n  substep-test.sh, notes references to the old\n  `--scope code,bot|por` form; delete `notes/fix-todo.py`\n- 0.53.0 close-out\n\n# References\n\n[1]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/commit/1e7c979e5458 \"1e7c979e5458189e4a5f380b18acd81d75ffe68b\"\n[2]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/commit/48b79876ef3f \"48b79876ef3f8f421eee81a63bb9937611558734\"\n[3]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/commit/9a3e1605d453 \"9a3e1605d453eaff6f7a45e50174fdfaee9f7b48\"\n[4]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/commit/61454c56229a \"61454c56229ac37afd89ab8bbcb7d2947eb9465c\"\n[5]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/commit/90584bfbd171 \"90584bfbd1710d9c4a5db6b93902b57c33875f6b\"\n[6]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/commit/9ba2b672a29f \"9ba2b672a29fa1dcd3aa9f5324ff18ea4b769f73\""
```json
}
```

```
36	2. **single-field `options_flags` leaves → `value` field.**
37	   `0.47.0` introduced the convention (single-field leaf names
38	   its field `value`, declares the flag via `#[arg(long = "…")]`,
39	   so consumers read `args.<leaf>.value` not `args.<leaf>.<leaf>`)
40	   on the new `squash` leaf. Sweep the pre-existing single-field
41	   leaves to match: `scope`, `repo`, `dry_run`, `private`,
42	   `account`, `config`, `use_template` + their consumers
43	   (`init.rs`, tests).
44	3. **Investigate `linkme` for subcommand registration.**
45	   Distributed-slice registry — each subcommand registers itself
46	   at link time; `main.rs` discovers them via the slice rather
47	   than matching a `Commands` enum. Reduces per-subcommand
48	   touchpoints from 3 (mod decl + enum variant + match arm) to 1
49	   (registration). Costs: loses compile-time exhaustiveness
50	   (missing registration = runtime gap); help-output ordering
51	   depends on link order unless sorted; macro-magic dependency.
52	   Revisit once the `0.50.0` trait sweep's per-arm cost has been
53	   felt under real "add a subcommand" load.
54	   <https://github.com/dtolnay/linkme>
55	4. **Investigate `inventory` as `linkme` alternative.** Same
56	   shape as `linkme` — runtime-iterable registry populated by
57	   `inventory::submit!` per subcommand. Trade-offs mirror
58	   linkme's. Pick one if/when the trait sweep's match becomes
59	   the felt bottleneck.
60	   <https://github.com/dtolnay/inventory>
61	5. **Drop `-R`/`--repo` from `CommonArgs` once `-s`/`--scope` is
62	   established.**
63	   - `0.49.0-2.2` kept `-R` (single path) alongside the new `-s`
64	     (roles, composes with `-R` as workspace root) as a
65	     backwards-compat alias for the migration period.
66	   - Once users / scripts have moved, drop `-R` — `--scope` then
67	     covers both path and role forms (`parse_scope` already
68	     handles both; only `parse_scope_roles` would go away).
69	6. **`-s <path>` and `-s <path>,roles` workspace-root override.**
70	   - Today `-s` is keyword-only (`parse_scope_roles` rejects
71	     paths, pointing users at `-R`).
72	   - Future: have `parse_scope_roles` accept a comma list mixing
73	     one path + role keywords, where the path is the workspace
74	     root.
75	   - `vc-x1 chid -s ../foo,bot,code` would resolve to `[../foo,
76	     ../foo/.claude]` (today `-R ../foo -s code,bot`).
77	   - Decisions to make at design time: comma-list syntax, error
78	     cases (multiple paths, path without roles vs
79	     path-as-`Scope::Single`), and whether to also accept bare
80	     `-s <path>` as a synonym for `-R <path>`.
```

```json
  "new_string": "# Regex for a leading `N. ` on a line — `\\d+` is one or more digits,\n# `\\.` is a literal dot, `\\s` is a whitespace character.\nNUM_RE = re.compile(r\"^(\\d+)\\.\\s\")\n\n\ndef renumber(content: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Walk `## Todo` and `## Bugs`, renumber entries, shift continuations.\"\"\"\n    # Split the file into lines, walk them, build the new file line-by-line\n    # in `out`, then rejoin at the end.\n    lines = content.split(\"\\n\")\n    out: list[str] = []\n\n    # State carried across the loop:\n    section: str | None = None  # current `## Heading`, or None outside any\n    entry_num = 0               # running entry count within `section`\n    shift = 0                   # for the most-recent entry's continuation\n                                # lines: how many spaces to add (positive)\n                                # or remove (negative) from their indent so\n                                # they stay aligned under the new prefix.\n\n    for line in lines:\n        # Section heading — entering a new section; reset counters.\n        if line.startswith(\"## \"):\n            section = line.strip()\n            entry_num = 0\n            shift = 0\n            out.append(line)\n            continue\n\n        # Only the two numbered sections need renumbering.\n        if section in (\"## Todo\", \"## Bugs\"):\n            # Entry first line: starts with `N. `. Renumber to the next\n            # sequential value and record the prefix-width delta for the\n            # entry's continuation lines that follow.\n            m = NUM_RE.match(line)\n            if m:\n                old_prefix = m.group(0)            # e.g. \"9. \"  or \"10. \"\n                entry_num += 1\n                new_prefix = f\"{entry_num}. \"      # e.g. \"10. \" or \"9. \"\n                out.append(new_prefix + line[len(old_prefix):])\n                shift = len(new_prefix) - len(old_prefix)\n                continue\n\n            # Continuation line under the current entry. If the entry's\n            # prefix changed width (shift != 0), pad or trim the leading\n            # spaces by the same amount so the text stays aligned under\n            # the new prefix. Blank lines pass through (no indent to fix).\n            if shift != 0 and line.strip() != \"\":\n                leading = len(line) - len(line.lstrip(\" \"))\n                new_leading = max(0, leading + shift)\n                out.append(\" \" * new_leading + line.lstrip(\" \"))\n                continue\n\n        # Everything else (blank lines, lines outside the two numbered\n        # sections, continuation lines when shift == 0) passes through\n        # unchanged.\n        out.append(line)\n\n    return \"\\n\".join(out)"
}
```

> DEVELOPER

I tweaked the comments and a couple of questions, see lines ending with (?)

> AGENT

**Line 52-53 — `out.append(line)` vs `out.append(section)`**: they only differ when a heading has trailing whitespace (`line` keeps it, `section` strips it). Today's todo.md has no trailing-space headings, so it's a non-issue in practice. The bot leans `line` though, because the script's job is renumbering — touching unrelated whitespace is a separate concern (and a better fit for a future `validate-todo` that audits whole-file hygiene). Preserve, don't silently normalize. So: keep `line`.

**Line 72 — "Assumes no empty lines between numbered items"**: no, blank lines are handled. A blank line fails the `line.strip() != ""` guard, falls through past the continuation branch, and lands on the unconditional `out.append(line)` at the bottom of the loop — passed through unchanged. `shift` carries forward across the blank line and is reset only by the next entry line (which sets a new `shift`) or the next `## ` heading. So:

```
9. **foo**           ← sets shift = +1 (was 8.)
   continuation      ← shift in effect → 3 spaces → 4 spaces
                     ← blank, passes through
10. **bar**          ← shift recomputed for this entry
```

Works correctly.

Good catch. The script's `^(\d+)\.\s` only knows "starts with a digit at column 0" — anything else in the section that happens to fit that shape would match too. The fragility is real, even if today's prose dodges it by luck.

Indenting intro prose by 1 space is the clean fix: CommonMark strips up to 3 leading spaces on paragraphs (no visual effect), and the `^` anchor never matches a space-prefixed line. Going to:

- Indent the `## Bugs` intro paragraph (lines after `## Bugs` heading) by 1.

- Mirror the rule in `fix-todo.py`'s docstring.

> DEVELOPER

We need to document the why and where indentation is needed and should it be 1, 2 or 3 spaces?

> AGENT

Two pieces, addressing each:

**How much indent?** **1 space.**

Rationale:
- The convention's job is to dodge the regex (`^\d+\.\s`), not to add visual emphasis. Minimum is the principled answer when more provides no functional benefit.
- 1 space is visually distinct from anything else in the file:
  - 0 spaces → entry first line.
  - 3 spaces → continuation under 1-digit entry, **and** matches today's intro indent — a reader can confuse prose for entry-continuation.
  - 4 spaces → continuation under 2-digit entry.
  - 1 space → unique to intro prose; the oddness itself signals "this means something" (and the convention prose explains what).
- Easier rule to remember: "1 space, no more, no less" beats "≥1 but use 3 to match the example list."

**Where to document?**

Two homes, each load-bearing:

- **`fix-todo.py` docstring** — for someone reading the script, explains why the script's regex assumes column-0 only. Already there; just trim "at least one" to "exactly one" and tighten the WHY.
- **`## Todo` convention prose** — for someone editing the file, explains the rule and points at the script as the reason. Future authors who add a new intro paragraph need to know.

Concretely, three edits:

1. Re-indent `## Todo` intro paragraphs (currently 3 spaces) → 1 space.
2. Re-indent `## Bugs` intro paragraph (currently 3 spaces from my last edit) → 1 space.
3. Add a sentence to the `## Todo` convention prose: "Intro paragraphs in `## Todo` and `## Bugs` indent **1 space** at the start of every line so they don't match the renumber script's entry regex (`^\d+\.\s`); see `notes/fix-todo.py`."
4. Tighten `fix-todo.py` docstring to "exactly one leading space" (and explain why minimum is right).

Proceed?

```
1	//! The `sync` subcommand: fetch + classify + (optionally) rebase /
2	//! fast-forward a workspace's repos against their remotes.
3	//!
4	//! Default is `--check` (fetch + report, error if any repo needs
5	//! action); `--no-check` applies. On any failure every repo is
6	//! reverted to its pre-sync op via `jj op restore`.
7	//!
8	//! `current_op_id` / `op_restore` are `pub(crate)` so `push` reuses
9	//! the same snapshot-and-restore pattern.

22	/// Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.
23	///
24	/// Default is `--check`: fetch + classify + report, **error** if any
25	/// repo is `behind` or `diverged`. Pass `--no-check` to actually
26	/// rebase / fast-forward. On any failure the starting state of every
27	/// repo is restored via `jj op restore`.
28	///
29	/// Scripts and automation should pass `--check` or `--no-check`
30	/// explicitly rather than relying on the default — defaults can shift,
31	/// explicit flags lock in the contract.
32	///
33	/// Repo set is resolved from `--scope`:
34	///
35	/// - Explicit `--scope=code|bot|code,bot|<path>` — keyword forms
36	///   resolve via the project root's `.vc-config.toml`; the path
37	///   form syncs that one repo (single-repo mode).
38	/// - No `--scope` — workspace-default scope:
39	///   - dual workspace (`.vc-config.toml` with `other-repo`) → `code,bot`
40	///   - single-repo workspace (`.vc-config.toml`, no `other-repo`) → `code`
41	///   - POR (no `.vc-config.toml`) → cwd as a single-repo target
42	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
43	pub struct SyncArgs {
44	    /// Verify only — fetch + classify; error if any repo needs action.
45	    /// This is the default; pass explicitly in scripts.
46	    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = "no_check")]
47	    pub check: bool,
48	
49	    /// Apply — fetch + classify, then rebase / fast-forward as needed.
50	    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = "check")]
51	    pub no_check: bool,
52	
53	    /// Suppress all informational output (exit code signals result)
54	    #[arg(short, long)]
55	    pub quiet: bool,
56	
57	    /// Bookmark to sync in each repo
58	    #[arg(long, default_value = "main")]
59	    pub bookmark: String,
60	
61	    /// Remote to sync against
62	    #[arg(long, default_value = "origin")]
63	    pub remote: String,
64	
65	    /// Which repo(s) of the workspace to sync.
66	    ///
67	    /// `SCOPE=code|bot|code,bot|<path>`:
68	    ///
69	    /// - `code` — sync only the app repo.
70	    /// - `bot` — sync only the bot repo (errors if no bot repo
71	    ///   is configured).
72	    /// - `code,bot` — sync both repos.
73	    /// - `<path>` (`./X`, `/X`, `~/X`) — single-repo mode; sync
74	    ///   only that repo, ignoring `.vc-config.toml`.
75	    ///
76	    /// Default depends on workspace state: dual workspace →
77	    /// `code,bot`; single-repo workspace → `code`; POR → cwd.
78	    #[arg(
79	        short = 's',
80	        long,
```

```
13	use jj_lib::hex_util::encode_reverse_hex;

17	use jj_lib::revset::{
18	    RevsetAliasesMap, RevsetDiagnostics, RevsetExtensions, RevsetParseContext,
19	    RevsetWorkspaceContext, SymbolResolver,

26	/// Result of parsing positional `..` notation.
27	///
28	/// Counts are `Some(0)` for closed sides and `None` for open (dotted) sides.
29	/// Open sides become unlimited unless a positional count is applied later.
30	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
31	pub struct DotSpec {
32	    /// The bare revision (without `..`).
33	    pub rev: String,
34	    /// Descendant count: `None` = open (dotted), `Some(0)` = closed.
35	    pub desc_count: Option<usize>,
36	    /// Ancestor count: `None` = open (dotted), `Some(0)` = closed.
37	    pub anc_count: Option<usize>,
38	}
39	
40	/// Parse a positional REV string for `..` notation.
41	///
42	/// Returns the bare revision with open/closed counts per side.
43	/// `None` means the side had dots (open for expansion);
44	/// `Some(0)` means no dots on that side (closed).
45	/// Actual counts are applied later from positional args.
46	pub fn parse_dot_rev(rev: &str) -> DotSpec {
47	    if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..").and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix("..")) {
48	        DotSpec {
49	            rev: inner.to_string(),
50	            desc_count: None,
51	            anc_count: None,
52	        }
53	    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..") {
54	        DotSpec {
55	            rev: inner.to_string(),
56	            desc_count: None,
57	            anc_count: Some(0),
58	        }
59	    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_suffix("..") {
60	        DotSpec {
61	            rev: inner.to_string(),
62	            desc_count: Some(0),
63	            anc_count: None,
64	        }
65	    } else {
66	        DotSpec {
67	            rev: rev.to_string(),
68	            desc_count: Some(0),
69	            anc_count: Some(0),
70	        }
71	    }
72	}
73	
74	/// Resolve positional and flag args into a `DotSpec` ready for `collect_ids`.
75	///
76	/// Handles: dot notation parsing, flag vs positional precedence,
77	/// count-means-total semantics (subtracts 1 for anchor).
78	pub fn resolve_spec(
79	    pos_rev: Option<&str>,
80	    pos_count: Option<usize>,
81	    flag_rev: &str,
82	    flag_limit: Option<usize>,
83	    default_rev: &str,
84	) -> DotSpec {
85	    let flag_rev_set = flag_rev != default_rev;
86	    let rev_str = if flag_rev_set {
87	        flag_rev
88	    } else {
89	        pos_rev.unwrap_or(default_rev) // OK: positional absent → fall back to CLI default
90	    };
91	    let mut spec = parse_dot_rev(rev_str);
92	
93	    let count = if flag_limit.is_some() {
94	        flag_limit
95	    } else {
96	        pos_count
97	    };
98	
99	    if let Some(c) = count {
100	        let n = c.saturating_sub(1);
101	        let both_open = spec.desc_count.is_none() && spec.anc_count.is_none();
102	        if both_open {
103	            // Split budget across both sides: ancestors get the extra
104	            let desc_n = n / 2;
105	            let anc_n = n - desc_n;
106	            spec.desc_count = Some(desc_n);
107	            spec.anc_count = Some(anc_n);
108	        } else {
109	            if spec.desc_count.is_none() {
110	                spec.desc_count = Some(n);
111	            }
112	            if spec.anc_count.is_none() {
113	                spec.anc_count = Some(n);
114	            }
115	            // bare `x 5` → treat as `x.. 5` (ancestors)
116	            if spec.desc_count == Some(0) && spec.anc_count == Some(0) {
117	                spec.anc_count = Some(n);
118	            }
119	        }
120	    }
121	
122	    spec
123	}
124	
125	/// Run a shell command. Returns stdout on success.
126	///
127	/// Logs the command at `debug!` and the process streams as follows:
128	/// - **stderr at `debug!`** on success — jj's chatter (`Moved 1 bookmarks
129	///   to …`, `Rebased N commits`, `Nothing changed.`) is hidden by default
130	///   and surfaced with `-v`. The debug formatter indents each line two
131	///   spaces so subprocess output sits visually under the caller's own
132	///   `info!` line in `-v` mode.
133	/// - **stdout at `debug!`** — callers usually consume stdout as data
134	///   (bookmark lists, commit IDs, etc.), and `info!` would flood the user
135	///   with machine-readable output they didn't ask for.
136	/// - **Failures propagate as `Err`** carrying the stderr; the caller's
137	///   error handler (`main::run_command`) surfaces it at `error!`.
138	pub fn run(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
139	    let args_str = args.join(" ");
140	    debug!("$ {cmd} {args_str}");
141	    trace!("cwd: {}", cwd.display());
142	    let output = std::process::Command::new(cmd)
143	        .args(args)
144	        .current_dir(cwd)
145	        .output()
146	        .map_err(|e| format!("failed to run {cmd}: {e}"))?;
147	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();
148	    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string();
149	    if !stdout.is_empty() {
150	        debug!("{stdout}");
151	    }
152	    if !output.status.success() {
153	        return Err(format!("{cmd} {args_str} failed: {stderr}").into());
154	    }
155	    if !stderr.is_empty() {
156	        debug!("{stderr}");
157	    }
158	    Ok(stdout)
159	}
160	
161	/// Create a directory (and parents). Logs at debug level.
162	pub fn mkdir_p(path: &Path) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
163	    debug!("mkdir -p {}", path.display());
164	    std::fs::create_dir_all(path)?;
165	    Ok(())
166	}
167	
168	/// Write content to a file. Logs at debug level.
169	pub fn write_file(path: &Path, content: &str) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
170	    debug!("write {}", path.display());
171	    std::fs::write(path, content)?;
172	    Ok(())
173	}
174	
175	/// Display a prompt, read a line from stdin, and log both together.
176	/// Returns the trimmed response.
177	pub fn prompt(msg: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
178	    use std::io::Write;
179	    eprint!("{msg}");
180	    std::io::stderr().flush()?;
181	    let mut response = String::new();
182	    std::io::stdin().read_line(&mut response)?;
183	    let trimmed = response.trim().to_string();
184	    info!("{msg}{trimmed}");
185	    Ok(trimmed)
186	}
187	
188	/// Wrap text in ANSI bold escape codes.
189	pub fn bold(s: &str) -> String {
190	    format!("\x1b[1m{s}\x1b[0m")
191	}
192	
193	/// Bold only the first line of a multi-line string.
194	pub fn bold_first_line(s: &str) -> String {
195	    if let Some(pos) = s.find('\n') {
196	        format!("\x1b[1m{}\x1b[0m{}", &s[..pos], &s[pos..])
197	    } else {
198	        bold(s)
199	    }
200	}
201	
202	/// Indent all lines after the first by `n` spaces.
203	///
204	/// Returns the string unchanged if `n == 0` or there is only one line.
205	pub fn indent_body(s: &str, n: usize) -> String {
206	    if n == 0 {
207	        return s.to_string();
208	    }
209	    let pad = " ".repeat(n);
210	    let mut lines = s.lines();
211	    let mut result = match lines.next() {
212	        Some(first) => first.to_string(),
213	        None => return String::new(),
214	    };
215	    for line in lines {
216	        result.push('\n');
217	        if !line.is_empty() {
218	            result.push_str(&pad);
219	        }
220	        result.push_str(line);
221	    }
222	    result
223	}
224	
225	/// Extract the ochid trailer value from a commit description, if present.
226	pub fn extract_ochid(commit: &Commit) -> Option<String> {
227	    for line in commit.description().lines().rev() {
228	        let trimmed = line.trim();
229	        if let Some(value) = trimmed.strip_prefix("ochid:") {
230	            return Some(value.trim().to_string());
231	        }
232	    }
233	    None
234	}
235	
236	/// Local bookmark names pointing exactly at `commit_id`, space-separated.
237	///
238	/// Returns an empty string when no local bookmark targets the commit. Remote
239	/// bookmarks are not included — that's a separate display concern.
240	pub fn format_bookmarks_at(repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>, commit_id: &CommitId) -> String {
241	    let view = repo.view();
242	    let names: Vec<String> = view
243	        .local_bookmarks_for_commit(commit_id)
244	        .map(|(name, _)| name.as_str().to_string())
245	        .collect();
246	    names.join(" ")
247	}
248	
249	/// Format: changeID ochid(padded) [bookmarks |] first-line.
250	///
251	/// ochid column is left-padded to `width` characters. If no ochid, a blank
252	/// placeholder of that width is used. When `bookmarks` is non-empty it is
253	/// inserted before the title with a `|` separator (mirroring jj's
254	/// `Parent commit (@-): <chid> <hash> <bookmark> | <title>` style).
255	pub fn format_commit_with_ochid(commit: &Commit, width: usize, bookmarks: &str) -> String {
256	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
257	    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
258	    let ochid = extract_ochid(commit).unwrap_or_default(); // OK: no ochid trailer → empty string
259	    let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: obvious
260	    let title = if first_line.is_empty() {
261	        "(no description set)"
262	    } else {
263	        first_line
264	    };
265	    if bookmarks.is_empty() {
266	        format!("{change_short}  {ochid:<width$}  {title}")
267	    } else {
268	        format!("{change_short}  {ochid:<width$}  {bookmarks} | {title}")
269	    }
270	}
271	
272	/// Format: just the short changeID.
273	pub fn format_chid(commit: &Commit) -> String {
274	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
275	    change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)].to_string()
276	}
277	
278	/// Format: changeID commitID [bookmarks |] first-line (single line).
279	///
280	/// When `bookmarks` is non-empty it is inserted before the title with a `|`
281	/// separator (mirroring jj's `<chid> <hash> <bookmark> | <title>` style).
282	pub fn format_commit_short(commit: &Commit, bookmarks: &str) -> String {
283	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
284	    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
285	    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
286	    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
287	    let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: obvious
288	    let title = if first_line.is_empty() {
289	        "(no description set)"
290	    } else {
291	        first_line
292	    };
293	    if bookmarks.is_empty() {
294	        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {title}")
295	    } else {
296	        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {bookmarks} | {title}")
297	    }
298	}
299	
300	/// Format: changeID commitID [bookmarks |] first-line, then remaining description lines.
301	///
302	/// Same header rules as `format_commit_short`; body lines follow unchanged.
303	pub fn format_commit_full(commit: &Commit, bookmarks: &str) -> String {
304	    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
305	    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
306	    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
307	    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
308	    let desc = commit.description();
309	    if desc.is_empty() {
310	        return if bookmarks.is_empty() {
311	            format!("{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)")
312	        } else {
313	            format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {bookmarks} | (no description set)")
314	        };
315	    }
316	    let mut lines = desc.lines();
317	    let first_line = lines.next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: obvious
318	    let mut result = if bookmarks.is_empty() {
319	        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}")
320	    } else {
321	        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {bookmarks} | {first_line}")
322	    };
323	    for line in lines {
324	        result.push('\n');
325	        result.push_str(line);
326	    }
327	    result
328	}
329	
330	/// Collect commit IDs for `..x..` (both directions) or any dot notation.
331	///
332	/// Returns `(commit_ids, anchor_index)` — the IDs in display order
333	/// (descendants closest to anchor, anchor, ancestors closest to anchor)
334	/// and the index of the anchor commit within the list.
335	///
336	/// - `desc_count`: `Some(n)` = n closest descendants, `None` = all, `Some(0)` = none
337	/// - `anc_count`: `Some(n)` = n closest ancestors, `None` = all, `Some(0)` = none
338	pub fn collect_ids(
339	    workspace: &Workspace,
340	    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
341	    rev: &str,
342	    desc_count: Option<usize>,
343	    anc_count: Option<usize>,
344	) -> Result<(Vec<CommitId>, usize), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
345	    let anchor_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, rev)?;
346	    if anchor_ids.is_empty() {
347	        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{rev}'").into());
348	    }
349	    let anchor_id = &anchor_ids[0];
350	    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
351	
352	    // Descendants (closest to anchor)
353	    let mut desc_ids: Vec<CommitId> = Vec::new();
354	    if desc_count != Some(0) {
355	        let descendant_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &format!("{rev}::"))?;
356	        desc_ids = descendant_ids
357	            .into_iter()
358	            .filter(|id| *id != root_commit_id && *id != *anchor_id)
359	            .collect();
360	        if let Some(n) = desc_count {
361	            let start = desc_ids.len().saturating_sub(n);
362	            desc_ids = desc_ids[start..].to_vec();
363	        }
364	    }
365	
366	    // Ancestors (closest to anchor)
367	    let mut anc_ids: Vec<CommitId> = Vec::new();
368	    if anc_count != Some(0) {
369	        let ancestor_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &format!("::{rev}"))?;
370	        let limit = anc_count.unwrap_or(usize::MAX); // OK: no --ancestors limit → unbounded
371	        let mut count = 0;
372	        for commit_id in ancestor_ids {
373	            if commit_id == root_commit_id || commit_id == *anchor_id {
374	                continue;
375	            }
376	            anc_ids.push(commit_id);
377	            count += 1;
378	            if count >= limit {
379	                break;
380	            }
381	        }
382	    }
383	
384	    let anchor_index = desc_ids.len();
385	    let mut result = desc_ids;
386	    result.push(anchor_id.clone());
387	    result.extend(anc_ids);
388	    Ok((result, anchor_index))
389	}
390	
391	/// Header style between repos in multi-repo output.
392	#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
393	pub enum Header {
394	    /// Show `deco path deco` header per repo.
395	    Label(String),
396	    /// No header at all.
397	    None,
398	}
399	
400	/// Resolve `-l` / `-L` flags into a `Header`.
401	///
402	/// `-L` (no header) takes precedence over `-l` (label decoration).
403	pub fn resolve_header(label: &str, suppress: bool) -> Header {
404	    if suppress {
405	        Header::None
406	    } else {
407	        Header::Label(label.to_string())
408	    }
409	}
410	
411	/// Run a closure once per resolved repo, with optional header decoration between repos.
412	///
413	/// Takes an already-resolved `&[PathBuf]` — the caller is responsible
414	/// for any flag-driven expansion (see `resolve_repos`). Continues past
415	/// errors, printing them to stderr, and returns an error if any
416	/// repo's body failed.
417	pub fn for_each_repo<F>(
418	    repos: &[PathBuf],
419	    header: &Header,
420	    mut body: F,
421	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>
422	where
423	    F: FnMut(&Workspace, &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>,
424	{
425	    let multi = repos.len() > 1;
426	    let mut first = true;
427	    let mut errors: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
428	
429	    for repo_path in repos {
430	        if multi {
431	            match header {
432	                Header::Label(deco) => {
433	                    if !first {
434	                        info!("");
435	                    }
436	                    info!(
437	                        "{}",
438	                        bold(&format!("{deco} {} {deco}", repo_path.display()))
439	                    );
440	                }
441	                Header::None => {}
442	            }
443	        }
444	        first = false;
445	
446	        match load_repo(repo_path) {
447	            Ok((workspace, repo)) => {
448	                if let Err(e) = body(&workspace, &repo) {
449	                    error!("({}): {e}", repo_path.display());
450	                    errors.push(format!("{}: {e}", repo_path.display()));
451	                }
452	            }
453	            Err(e) => {
454	                error!("({}): {e}", repo_path.display());
455	                errors.push(format!("{}: {e}", repo_path.display()));
456	            }
457	        }
458	    }
459	
460	    if errors.is_empty() {
461	        Ok(())
462	    } else {
463	        Err(format!("{} repo(s) had errors", errors.len()).into())
464	    }
465	}
466	
467	pub fn load_repo(
468	    path: &Path,
469	) -> Result<(Workspace, Arc<ReadonlyRepo>), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
470	    let config = StackedConfig::with_defaults();
471	    let settings = UserSettings::from_config(config)?;
472	    let store_factories = StoreFactories::default();
473	    let working_copy_factories = default_working_copy_factories();
474	
475	    let workspace = Workspace::load(&settings, path, &store_factories, &working_copy_factories)?;
476	    let repo = workspace.repo_loader().load_at_head().block_on()?;
477	
478	    Ok((workspace, repo))
479	}
480	
481	/// Parse and evaluate a revset string, returning commit IDs.
482	pub fn resolve_revset(
483	    workspace: &Workspace,
484	    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
485	    revset_str: &str,
486	) -> Result<Vec<CommitId>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
487	    let aliases_map = RevsetAliasesMap::new();
488	    let fileset_aliases_map = FilesetAliasesMap::new();
489	    let extensions = RevsetExtensions::default();
490	    let workspace_root = workspace.workspace_root().to_path_buf();
491	    let path_converter = RepoPathUiConverter::Fs {
492	        cwd: workspace_root.clone(),
493	        base: workspace_root,
494	    };
495	    let workspace_name = workspace.workspace_name();
496	    let workspace_ctx = RevsetWorkspaceContext {
497	        path_converter: &path_converter,
498	        workspace_name,
499	    };
500	    let context = RevsetParseContext {
501	        aliases_map: &aliases_map,
502	        local_variables: HashMap::new(),
503	        user_email: "",
504	        date_pattern_context: chrono::Utc::now().fixed_offset().into(),
505	        default_ignored_remote: None,
506	        fileset_aliases_map: &fileset_aliases_map,
507	        use_glob_by_default: false,
508	        extensions: &extensions,
509	        workspace: Some(workspace_ctx),
510	    };
511	
512	    let mut diagnostics = RevsetDiagnostics::new();
513	    let expression = jj_lib::revset::parse(&mut diagnostics, revset_str, &context)?;
514	
515	    let no_extensions: &[Box<dyn jj_lib::revset::SymbolResolverExtension>] = &[];
516	    let symbol_resolver = SymbolResolver::new(repo.as_ref(), no_extensions);
517	    let resolved = expression.resolve_user_expression(repo.as_ref(), &symbol_resolver)?;
518	    let revset = resolved.evaluate(repo.as_ref())?;
519	
520	    let mut commit_ids = Vec::new();
521	    for result in revset.iter() {
522	        commit_ids.push(result?);
523	    }
524	    Ok(commit_ids)
525	}
526	
527	/// Scan `jj bookmark list -a <name>` output for non-tracking remote refs.
528	///
529	/// Tracked remotes appear indented (`  @origin: ...`); non-tracking remotes
530	/// appear at column 0 as `<bookmark>@<remote>: ...`. Returns the
531	/// non-tracking remote name if any is found.
532	pub fn find_non_tracking_remote(list_output: &str, bookmark: &str) -> Option<String> {
533	    let prefix = format!("{bookmark}@");
534	    for line in list_output.lines() {
535	        if line.starts_with(&prefix)
536	            && let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix(&prefix)
537	            && let Some((remote, _)) = rest.split_once(':')
538	        {
539	            return Some(remote.to_string());
540	        }
541	    }
542	    None
543	}
544	
545	/// Verify all remote refs for `bookmark` in `repo` are tracked.
546	///
547	/// Returns `Err` with the exact `jj bookmark track …` remediation command if
548	/// any non-tracking remote ref is found. Used as a preflight by repo-modifying
549	/// commands (sync, push, finalize) and as a post-condition assertion by setup
550	/// commands (init, clone, test-fixture).
551	pub fn verify_tracking(repo: &Path, bookmark: &str) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
552	    let repo_str = repo.to_string_lossy();
553	    let cwd = Path::new(".");
554	    let all = run(
555	        "jj",
556	        &["bookmark", "list", "-a", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str],
557	        cwd,
558	    )?;
559	    if let Some(remote) = find_non_tracking_remote(&all, bookmark) {
560	        return Err(format!(
561	            "bookmark '{bookmark}' has non-tracking remote '{bookmark}@{remote}' — \
562	             run `jj bookmark track {bookmark} --remote={remote} -R {repo_str}` to fix"
563	        )
564	        .into());
565	    }
566	    Ok(())
567	}
568	
569	/// Walk up from `start` to find the workspace root.
570	///
571	/// The workspace root is the directory whose `.vc-config.toml` has
572	/// `path = "/"`. Returns `None` if no such config is found up to the
573	/// filesystem root — the caller is in a "plain old repo" (POR) with
574	/// no vc-x1 workspace metadata.
575	pub fn find_workspace_root_from(start: &Path) -> Option<PathBuf> {
576	    let mut cur = start.to_path_buf();
577	    loop {
578	        let cfg = cur.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE);
579	        if let Ok(map) = toml_simple::toml_load(&cfg)
580	            && toml_simple::toml_get(&map, "workspace.path").map(String::as_str) == Some("/")
581	        {
582	            return Some(cur);
583	        }
584	        cur = cur.parent()?.to_path_buf();
585	    }
586	}
587	
588	/// Cwd-anchored wrapper over [`find_workspace_root_from`].
589	pub fn find_workspace_root() -> Option<PathBuf> {
590	    let cur = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;
591	    find_workspace_root_from(&cur)
592	}
593	
594	/// Resolve the workspace's default `--scope`.
595	///
596	/// Reads `<workspace_root>/.vc-config.toml > [workspace] other-repo`:
597	///
598	/// - dual workspace (non-empty `other-repo`) → `Roles([Code, Bot])`
599	/// - single-repo workspace (missing / empty `other-repo`) → `Roles([Code])`
600	/// - POR (no workspace_root) → `Single(cwd)` — operate on cwd as a
601	///   one-off jj repo, ignoring any workspace context.
602	pub fn default_scope(workspace_root: Option<&Path>, cwd: &Path) -> Scope {
603	    let Some(root) = workspace_root else {
604	        return Scope::Single(cwd.to_path_buf());
605	    };
606	    let cfg = match toml_simple::toml_load(&root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE)) {
607	        Ok(c) => c,
608	        Err(_) => return Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code]),
609	    };
610	    match toml_simple::toml_get(&cfg, "workspace.other-repo") {
611	        Some(v) if !v.is_empty() => Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]),
612	        _ => Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code]),
613	    }
614	}
615	
616	/// Resolve a `Scope` to concrete repo paths.
617	///
618	/// `Roles(...)` arm:
619	///
620	/// - `Side::Code` → `workspace_root` (or cwd's `.` when `workspace_root` is None).
621	/// - `Side::Bot` → `workspace_root.join(other-repo)`.
622	///
623	/// Errors when `Side::Bot` is requested but the workspace doesn't define
624	/// one (POR or single-repo workspace).
625	///
626	/// `Single(p)` arm: returns `vec![p]` directly — no workspace lookup,
627	/// no `other-repo` resolution. The path is whatever the caller stored
628	/// (typically the value the user passed to `--scope=<path>`, or cwd
629	/// from `default_scope`'s POR branch); shell-style expansion (tilde,
630	/// `$VAR`) happens at the parser/consumer boundary, not here.
631	pub fn scope_to_repos(
632	    scope: &Scope,
633	    workspace_root: Option<&Path>,
634	) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
635	    let sides = match scope {
636	        Scope::Roles(v) => v,
637	        Scope::Single(p) => return Ok(vec![p.clone()]),
638	    };
639	    let mut repos = Vec::new();
640	    for side in sides {
641	        match side {
642	            Side::Code => repos.push(
643	                workspace_root
644	                    .map(Path::to_path_buf)
645	                    .unwrap_or_else(|| PathBuf::from(".")),
646	            ),
647	            Side::Bot => {
648	                let root = workspace_root.ok_or(
649	                    "--scope=bot: not in a vc-x1 workspace (no .vc-config.toml with path = \"/\") — drop --scope or use --scope=code",
650	                )?;
651	                let cfg = toml_simple::toml_load(&root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE))?;
652	                let other = toml_simple::toml_get(&cfg, "workspace.other-repo")
653	                    .filter(|v| !v.is_empty())
654	                    .ok_or(
655	                        "--scope=bot: no other-repo configured. Add `other-repo = \"…\"` to .vc-config.toml to enable dual-repo operations",
656	                    )?;
657	                repos.push(root.join(other));
658	            }
659	        }
660	    }
661	    Ok(repos)
662	}
663	
664	/// Resolve `CommonArgs.repo` + `CommonArgs.scope` into concrete repo paths.
665	///
666	/// The four `CommonArgs` consumers (`chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show`)
667	/// call this at the start of their body to translate the clap surface
668	/// into the `&[PathBuf]` that `for_each_repo` iterates. Behavior matches
669	/// today's defaults plus a composing rule for the new `--scope` flag:
670	///
671	/// - neither flag → `[.]` (today's no-arg default).
672	/// - `-R PATH` alone → `[PATH]` (today's `-R <path>` behavior;
673	///   workspace context not consulted).
674	/// - `-s ROLES` alone → resolves against `find_workspace_root()` —
675	///   the surrounding workspace if there is one, else POR
676	///   (`Side::Code` → `.`, `Side::Bot` → error).
677	/// - `-R PATH -s ROLES` → resolves with `PATH` as the workspace root
678	///   (overrides `find_workspace_root`). This is the composing case;
679	///   e.g. `chid -R ../foo -s bot` queries `../foo/.claude`.
680	///
681	/// `--scope` is keyword-only today (the `parse_scope_roles` value
682	/// parser rejects path forms); the planned `-s <path>` /
683	/// `-s <path>,roles` workspace-root override is a future feature.
684	pub fn resolve_repos(
685	    repo: Option<&Path>,
686	    scope: Option<&Scope>,
687	) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
688	    match (repo, scope) {
689	        (None, None) => Ok(vec![PathBuf::from(".")]),
690	        (Some(p), None) => Ok(vec![p.to_path_buf()]),
691	        (Some(p), Some(s)) => scope_to_repos(s, Some(p)),
692	        (None, Some(s)) => scope_to_repos(s, find_workspace_root().as_deref()),
693	    }
694	}
695	
696	/// Resolved shared params for the read-only commit-query subcommands
697	/// (`chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show`). Built once at the binary edge
698	/// from `CommonArgs` via `TryFrom`; each subcommand's `XxxParams`
699	/// embeds this.
700	///
701	/// - `spec` — resolved `DotSpec` (parsed `..` notation + per-side
702	///   count budget).
703	/// - `header` — resolved inter-repo `Header` (label vs none).
704	/// - `repos` — resolved repo paths (`-R` + `-s` → `Vec<PathBuf>`).
705	#[derive(Debug)]
706	pub struct CommonParams {
707	    pub spec: DotSpec,
708	    pub header: Header,
709	    pub repos: Vec<PathBuf>,
710	}
711	
712	impl TryFrom<&CommonArgs> for CommonParams {
713	    type Error = String;
714	
715	    /// Resolve clap `CommonArgs` into clap-free `CommonParams`: run
716	    /// `resolve_spec` / `resolve_header` / `resolve_repos` at the
717	    /// binary edge. Fallible because `resolve_repos` can fail
718	    /// (workspace lookup, path issues).
719	    fn try_from(a: &CommonArgs) -> Result<Self, String> {
720	        let spec = resolve_spec(a.pos_rev.as_deref(), a.pos_count, &a.revision, a.limit, "@");
721	        let header = resolve_header(&a.label, a.no_label);
722	        let repos = a.resolve_repos().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
723	        Ok(CommonParams {
724	            spec,
725	            header,
726	            repos,
727	        })
728	    }
729	}
730	
731	#[cfg(test)]
732	mod tests;
733	
```

> AGENT

Opener edits done: todo.md ladder marks 0.53.0-1 (current), Cargo bumped, task #1 completed and #2 in_progress.

Now I want to confirm the design choices before touching source. The cleanup is bigger than just removing `Scope::Single` — three real decisions:

## Decision 1 — `Scope` type after `Single` is gone

With only one variant left, the enum is half-cleanup. Three shapes:

- **(a) Single-variant enum** `Scope::Roles(Vec<Side>)` — minimal churn (every `match` site keeps its `Scope::Roles(v) => …` arm).
- **(b) Newtype struct** `pub struct Scope(pub Vec<Side>)` — drop the variant, accessors via `scope.0` or methods. Cleaner end state, more touch sites.
- **(c) Drop the type entirely**, use `Vec<Side>` everywhere — most cleanup but loses the "this Vec means roles" naming.

The bot leans **(b)**. The whole reason for the enum was distinguishing Roles from Single; without that distinction, the enum is vestigial. Naming the type via newtype keeps the call-site readability.

## Decision 2 — POR's `default_scope` return value

Today, `default_scope(workspace_root=None, cwd)` returns `Scope::Single(cwd)`. After `Single` goes away, two options:

- **POR returns `Roles([Code])`** — identical runtime behavior because `scope_to_repos(Code, workspace_root=None)` already falls back to `PathBuf::from(".")`. Drop the `cwd` parameter from `default_scope` since it's unused.
- Different shape — invent a new variant.

**Lean (i)** — it's a no-op behavior-wise; the migration is just changing what value is constructed.

## Decision 3 — `sync --scope <path>`

Today `vc-x1 sync` accepts `--scope ./foo` as a single-repo sync target. After path-in-scope dies, options:

- **Drop the path form entirely.** Users who want to sync a non-workspace repo can `cd && vc-x1 sync`. If a real use case appears, add `-R/--repo` to sync as a separate todo.
- **Add `-R/--repo` to sync now** to preserve the capability.

**Lean (i)** — drop. Sync's intent is "sync the workspace I'm in." If it turns out to matter, `-R` can land later (already implicit in the `-R/--scope` symmetry we've established for query commands).

OK to proceed on all three leans (newtype `Scope`, POR returns `Roles([Code])`, drop sync's path form)?

> AGENT

**"Roles-only" — what's left of `Scope`:**

Today the runtime `Scope` is an enum with two variants:
- `Roles(Vec<Side>)` — the role-bearing form (code / bot / both).
- `Single(PathBuf)` — the path-bearing form (operate on this one repo, ignore `.vc-config.toml`).

"Roles-only" = the `Single(PathBuf)` variant goes away. What remains carries role information only — a `Vec<Side>` wrapped in a newtype (`pub struct Scope(pub Vec<Side>)`).

The variant could disappear because paths are no longer expressible in `--scope` — they live on a separate flag (`-R/--repo`). So the runtime type no longer needs to model "is this a role-set or a path?"

**`--scope` value grammar after the cleanup — your second question:**

| Form | Accepted before? | Accepted after? |
|---|---|---|
| `code` | ✓ | ✓ |
| `bot` | ✓ | ✓ |
| `code,bot` | ✓ | ✓ |
| `bot,code` | ✓ | ✓ |
| `<path>` (`./X`, `/X`, `~/X`) | ✓ (today on `sync --scope`; `parse_scope_roles` rejects elsewhere) | **✗ dropped** |
| `por` | ✓ (on `init`/`clone` via `ScopeKind`) | **✗ dropped — replaced by `--por` boolean** |

So `--scope` accepts the four role-keyword forms only after `0.53.0`. No path, no topology. Path-only operation routes through `-R/--repo` (already on the query commands; not on sync today — `cd && vc-x1 sync` is the workaround). Topology choice routes through `--por`.

This matches the three-orthogonal-concerns split documented in `chores-11.md`'s `0.53.0-0` opener:
- **Path** → `-R/--repo`
- **Roles** → `-s/--scope` (the four keywords)
- **Topology** → `--por` boolean

Does that match your understanding, or is something off?

> DEVELOPER

Maybe sync should accept -R,--repo so you don't have to change directory to sync?

> AGENT

Agreed — that's a real UX regression to leave open. Adding `-R/--repo` to sync is small once `Scope` is settled, and it preserves today's "sync a non-workspace repo without cd" capability.

**Where it fits:** bundle into `0.53.0-2` (the Scope collapse + relocate substep). That substep already touches `sync.rs` to consume the relocated `Scope`; adding the `-R` field while there avoids splitting "sync edits" across two commits. Same semantics as CommonArgs's `-R`:

- `-R PATH` alone → sync the single repo at PATH.
- `-R PATH + -s ROLES` → use PATH as workspace root, resolve sides.
- `-s ROLES` alone → resolve against `find_workspace_root()` from cwd.
- Neither → cwd's workspace defaults.

Sync's repo resolution becomes a thin wrapper around the same compose logic CommonArgs uses (or `common::resolve_repos` outright if the defaults line up — minor refactor at most).

**Revised ladder:**

| Substep | Work |
|---|---|
| 0.53.0-1 | Delete `options_flags/scope.rs`; replace with `--por` boolean. Migrate init, clone, test_helpers. |
| 0.53.0-2 | Collapse `Scope` to roles-only; relocate to `options_flags/scope.rs`; **add `-R/--repo` to sync**. |
| 0.53.0-3 | Docs/script sweep. |
| 0.53.0 | Close-out. |

Cycle title at close-out probably becomes `refactor: clean up scope + todo list (0.53.0)` (unchanged from current opener). The sync `-R` addition is small enough to ride along as one bullet in `0.53.0-2`'s chores section, not a new design subsection.

OK to land this revised ladder and start `0.53.0-1`?

**Two paths to the same end state:**

| Path | Commits | Force-push? | History |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Rewrite** | rewrite -0 → 0.53.0; new commit for 0.54.0-0 | yes, on both repos | clean: 0.53.0 directly records the notes hygiene |
| **Forward-only** | new close-out commit for 0.53.0; new commit for 0.54.0-0 | no | 0.53.0-0 (planned-scope-cleanup title) + 0.53.0 (re-scope close-out section) — honest about the mid-cycle pivot |

Its job (reject path forms in\n  the roles slot) is moot once paths aren't a roles form.\n- `options_flags/scope.rs` (`ScopeKind`, `ScopeOption`,\n  `parse_scope_kind`, `ScopeParser`) — replaced by a\n  `PorFlag` leaf in `options_flags/`.\n\nThe deeper symmetric-`.vc-config.toml` refactor (todo #11)\nis independent and lands in `0.54.0`; keeping it separate\nkeeps each cycle reviewable.\n\n### Todo items the cycle absorbs\n\nDeletes:\n\n- #5 (was #4): \"Drop `-R/--repo` from `CommonArgs`\" —\n  premised on folding `<path>` into `-s`, rejected here.\n- #6 (was #5): \"`-s <path>` workspace-root override\" —\n  same premise, same fate.\n- #14 (was #13): \"init dual|por arg split\" — settled\n  with `--por` flag (a third shape neither half of the\n  entry considered).\n\nRewrites / light touch:\n\n- #2 (was #1): drop `scope` from the single-field\n  leaves sweep — `ScopeOption` is being deleted.\n- #13 (was #12): `--scope` sweep continuation —\n  `Single(_)` dogfood half drops out; only the\n  `finalize --scope` replacing `--repo` half survives.\n- #19 (was #18), #20 (was #19): push/clone\n  `--scope=...|<path>` flag designs — `<path>` form\n  drops out.\n- #21 (was #20): validate-desc/fix-desc `--scope` —\n  `Single(_)`-errors-here parenthetical moot.\n\nA new entry #1 (`vc-x1 fix-todo` / `validate-todo`)\ncaptures the future Rust replacement for the interim\n`notes/fix-todo.py`.\n\n### Notes-side prep done at -0\n\nThe cycle's todo edits needed clean ordinal references, so\nthe opener also lands a notes-convention change:\n\n- `## Todo` / `## Bugs` entries now use manual numbering\n  (`1.` `2.` …) rather than lazy `1.` everywhere. The raw\n  source is readable without rendering, at the cost of\n  diff churn on reorder. See the convention prose in\n  todo.md.\n- `notes/fix-todo.py` — interim Python script that\n  renumbers and shifts continuation indent on a delete /\n  reorder. Slated for Rust replacement (todo #1).\n\n### Ladder\n\n- 0.53.0-0 plan + version bump + notes convention +\n  fix-todo script + todo edits (current)\n- 0.53.0-1 collapse `src/scope.rs` to roles-only —\n  drop `Scope::Single`, `parse_scope_roles`; simplify\n  parser + helpers\n- 0.53.0-2 replace `ScopeKind`/`ScopeOption` with\n  `--por` flag — delete `options_flags/scope.rs`;\n  migrate init / clone / test_helpers\n- 0.53.0-3 docs/script sweep — README, ARCHITECTURE,\n  substep-test.sh, notes references to the old\n  `--scope code,bot|por` form; delete `notes/fix-todo.py`\n- 0.53.0 close-out",
```json
  "new_string": "## chore: todo renumber + fix-todo.py (0.53.0)\n\nNotes-side hygiene cycle. Originally opened (and committed\nunder the working title `refactor: clean up scope + todo\nlist`) as a multi-step scope CLI cleanup, but re-scoped at\nthe close-out boundary when the notes-side prep turned out\nto be a separable concern that landed cleanly on its own.\nThe scope CLI cleanup opens fresh as `0.54.0` with a\ndedicated plan; the captured design (three orthogonal\nconcerns: path on `-R`, roles on `-s`, topology on `--por`;\n`Scope` relocates into `options_flags/scope.rs`) carries\nforward to `0.54.0-0`'s chores section.\n\n### What landed\n\n- **Manual `N.` entry numbering** in `## Todo` and `## Bugs`\n  (was lazy `1.` everywhere). Each insert / delete / reorder\n  bumps numbers in lockstep; `notes/fix-todo.py` automates\n  this and shifts continuation-line indent across\n  prefix-width changes (3 ↔ 4 spaces).\n- **Intro prose indented 1 space** per line in `## Todo` and\n  `## Bugs` so it doesn't accidentally match the renumber\n  regex (`^\\d+\\.\\s`). Convention documented at the top of\n  `todo.md` (and mirrored in `notes/fix-todo.py`'s\n  docstring).\n- **44 entries** after 1 insert (#1 — future `vc-x1\n  fix-todo` Rust subcommand), 3 deletes (was #4/#5/#13 —\n  design decisions superseded their content), 4 rewrites\n  (was #12/#18/#19/#20 — `Single(_)` and `<path>` references\n  dropped to reflect 0.54.0's design).\n\n### Outcome — re-scope note\n\nThe cycle was opened bundling notes-side prep with the scope\nCLI cleanup. Mid-cycle, several observations pulled the two\napart:\n\n- The notes-side change (renumbering, fix-todo.py, intro\n  indent rule) was substantial enough on its own to warrant\n  a clean review.\n- The scope CLI cleanup grew during planning — sync gained\n  `-R/--repo`, the runtime `Scope` type wanted relocation\n  into `options_flags/`, the substep ladder re-ordered.\n  Each tweak compounded.\n- Keeping both in one cycle would muddy both narratives.\n\nClosing `0.53.0` here as notes hygiene only keeps the two\ncycles' reviews independent."
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/chores/chores-11.md",
  "old_string": "Closing `0.53.0` here as notes hygiene only keeps the two\ncycles' reviews independent.\n\n# References",
  "new_string": "Closing `0.53.0` here as notes hygiene only keeps the two\ncycles' reviews independent.\n\n## refactor: scope CLI cleanup (0.54.0-0)\n\nMulti-step. Today, two `--scope` flags coexist with\ndifferent value grammars: query-side `-s code|bot|code,bot`\n(which sides of an existing workspace) and creation-side\n`-s code,bot|por` (workspace topology). The cleanup factors\nthe three concerns apart onto three orthogonal flags:\n\n- **Path** — `-R/--repo PATH`. Always present (defaults to\n  cwd). Independent of role selection.\n- **Roles** — `-s/--scope code|bot|code,bot|bot,code`.\n  Single-purpose: which side(s) of an existing dual\n  workspace.\n- **Topology** — `--por` boolean. Creation-only (init,\n  clone). The `code,bot|por` grammar collapses to \"do\n  you want a `.claude` companion or not.\"\n\nAfter this cycle:\n\n- `Scope::Single(PathBuf)` — gone. `-R PATH` is the\n  path-only entry point.\n- `parse_scope_roles` — gone. Its job (reject path forms\n  in the roles slot) is moot once paths aren't a roles\n  form.\n- `options_flags/scope.rs` (`ScopeKind`, `ScopeOption`,\n  `parse_scope_kind`, `ScopeParser`) — replaced by a\n  `PorFlag` boolean leaf in `options_flags/`.\n- `src/scope.rs` — relocated into\n  `options_flags/scope.rs` (no longer ambiguous with the\n  deleted file). `Scope` becomes a newtype\n  `pub struct Scope(pub Vec<Side>)`; the `Single` variant\n  goes away.\n- `vc-x1 sync` gains `-R/--repo` for single-repo or\n  workspace-root override, parallel to `CommonArgs`'s\n  `-R`. Preserves today's `sync --scope <path>`\n  capability after path-in-scope drops.\n\nThe design was captured during `0.53.0`'s opener (committed\nunder the working title `refactor: clean up scope + todo\nlist` then re-scoped) and carries forward here. The deeper\nsymmetric-`.vc-config.toml` refactor (todo #11) is\nindependent and lands in a later cycle.\n\n### Ladder\n\n- 0.54.0-0 plan + version bump + chores opener (current)\n- 0.54.0-1 delete `options_flags/scope.rs`; replace with\n  `--por` boolean flag leaf; migrate init / clone /\n  test_helpers\n- 0.54.0-2 collapse `Scope` to roles-only (newtype over\n  `Vec<Side>`); relocate `src/scope.rs` into\n  `options_flags/scope.rs`; drop `parse_scope_roles`;\n  add `-R/--repo` to sync\n- 0.54.0-3 docs/script sweep — README, ARCHITECTURE,\n  substep-test.sh, notes references to the old\n  `--scope code,bot|por` form; delete\n  `notes/fix-todo.py`\n- 0.54.0 close-out\n\n### Per-step evaluation\n\nEffectiveness is evaluated after each substep. Possible\noutcomes at any step boundary:\n\n- Continue as planned.\n- Significantly modify the shape — recorded in a new\n  chores subsection at the next step.\n- Abandon: revert the cycle to a no-op with an\n  `### Outcome` note capturing what didn't fit.\n\n# References"
}
```

```
1	//! `vc-x1 clone` — clone a repo (URL or local path) into a workspace.
2	//!
3	//! - `--scope=code,bot` (default): dual-repo layout — clones code,
4	//!   derives bot source (`<source>.claude`), clones bot into
5	//!   `<target>/.claude`, creates the Claude Code symlink. Both
6	//!   sides must succeed.
7	//! - `--scope=por`: single repo into `<target>`. No `.claude/`,
8	//!   no symlink.
9	//!
10	//! TARGET shapes (all routed through `parse_target`): URL,
11	//! `owner/name` shorthand, or a local path (`./X`, `/X`, `~/X`,
12	//! `.`, `..`). Path-form is symmetric with `git clone /local/bare.git`
13	//! — useful for fixtures and CI scratch dirs.
14	//!
15	//! `clone_one` and `clone_dual` are `pub(crate)` so init's `-3`
16	//! reshape can reuse them for the "URL exists → clone" preflight
17	//! path (per `notes/chores/chores-08.md > init + clone redesign`).
18

33	pub struct CloneArgs {
34	    /// Source to clone — URL, owner/name shorthand, or local path.
35	    ///
36	    /// - URL: `git@host:owner/name(.git)?`, `https://...(.git)?`
37	    /// - owner/name shorthand: resolves to
38	    ///   `git@github.com:owner/name.git`
39	    /// - Local path: `./X`, `../X`, `/X`, `~/X`, `~`, `.`, `..`
40	    ///   (passed directly to `git clone`)
41	    #[arg(value_name = "TARGET", verbatim_doc_comment)]
42	    pub target: String,
43	
44	    /// Destination dir name in cwd [default: derived from TARGET]
45	    #[arg(value_name = "NAME")]
46	    pub name: Option<String>,
47	
48	    /// What to clone: `code,bot` (dual, default) or `por` (single).
49	    #[arg(
50	        long,
51	        short,
52	        value_name = "SCOPE",
53	        value_parser = parse_scope_kind,
54	        default_value = "code,bot"
55	    )]
56	    pub scope: ScopeKind,
57	
58	    /// Dry run — show what would be done without executing.
59	    #[arg(long)]
60	    pub dry_run: bool,
61	}

64	///
65	/// - `target`: the `TARGET` positional (URL, `owner/name`, or
66	///   local path).
67	/// - `name`: optional `NAME` positional override for the
68	///   destination dir.
69	/// - `scope`: `--scope` resolved (default `code,bot`).
70	/// - `dry_run`: `--dry-run`.
71	pub struct CloneParams {
72	    pub target: String,
73	    pub name: Option<String>,
74	    pub scope: ScopeKind,
75	    pub dry_run: bool,
76	}
```

```
78	impl From<&CloneArgs> for CloneParams {
79	    /// Convert clap-derived `CloneArgs` into the flat `CloneParams`
80	    /// (total — every field copies straight over).
81	    fn from(a: &CloneArgs) -> Self {
82	        Self {
83	            target: a.target.clone(),
84	            name: a.name.clone(),
85	            scope: a.scope,
86	            dry_run: a.dry_run,
87	        }
88	    }
89	}
90	
91	impl SubcommandRunner for CloneArgs {
92	    type Params = CloneParams;
93	
94	    /// Delegate to the existing `From<&CloneArgs>` impl above
95	    /// (total — never fails).
96	    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String> {
97	        Ok(CloneParams::from(self))
98	    }
99	
100	    /// Run the existing `clone_repo` op.
101	    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
102	        clone_repo(ctx, params)
103	    }
104	}
105	
106	/// Top-level clone driver.
107	///
108	/// - Resolves TARGET to a concrete clone source (URL or path).
109	/// - Determines destination dir name (`[NAME]` override or
110	///   `derive_name(TARGET)`).
111	/// - Pre-checks target dir doesn't exist.
112	/// - Dispatches to `clone_one` (POR) or `clone_dual` (code,bot).
113	///
114	/// `ctx` is unused today (clone reads neither user config nor the
115	/// `--log` path); it's present for the uniform subcommand-layer
116	/// signature.
117	pub fn clone_repo(_ctx: &Context, params: &CloneParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
118	    log::debug!("clone: enter");
119	
120	    let parsed = parse_target(&params.target)?;
121	    let source = match parsed {
122	        Target::Url(u) => u,
123	        Target::OwnerName(o, n) => resolve_url(&format!("{o}/{n}")),
124	        Target::Path(p) => p.to_str().ok_or("path is not valid UTF-8")?.to_string(),
125	        Target::BareName(n) => {
126	            return Err(format!(
127	                "'{n}' is a bare name — clone has no config-driven defaults; \
128	                 use 'owner/{n}', a full URL, or './{n}' for a local path"
129	            )
130	            .into());
131	        }
132	    };
133	
134	    let name = match &params.name {
135	        Some(n) => n.clone(),
136	        None => derive_name(&params.target)?,
137	    };
138	    let parent_dir = std::env::current_dir()?;
139	    let project_dir = parent_dir.join(&name);
140	
141	    if project_dir.exists() {
142	        return Err(format!("'{}' already exists", project_dir.display()).into());
143	    }
144	
145	    if params.dry_run {
146	        info!("Dry run — would execute:");
147	        match params.scope {
148	            ScopeKind::Por => {
149	                info!("  1. jj git clone --colocate {source} {name}");
150	            }
151	            ScopeKind::CodeBot => {
152	                let session_source = derive_session_url(&source);
153	                info!("  1. jj git clone --colocate {source} {name}");
154	                info!("  2. jj git clone --colocate {session_source} {name}/.claude");
155	                info!("  3. Create Claude Code symlink");
156	            }
157	        }
158	        return Ok(());
159	    }
160	
161	    run("jj", &["--version"], Path::new(".")).map_err(|_| "jj is not installed")?;
162	
163	    match params.scope {
164	        ScopeKind::Por => {
165	            clone_one(&source, &project_dir, &parent_dir)?;
166	            info!("");
167	            info!("Done! Project cloned to {}", project_dir.display());
168	            info!("  Code repo: {}", project_dir.display());
169	        }
170	        ScopeKind::CodeBot => clone_dual(&source, &project_dir, &parent_dir)?,
171	    }
172	
173	    log::debug!("clone: exit");
174	    Ok(())
175	}
176	
177	/// Clone a single repo via `jj git clone --colocate` and verify
178	/// bookmark tracking.
179	///
180	/// - `source` is the clone source (URL or local path).
181	/// - `target_dir` is the destination working repo location.
182	/// - `parent_dir` is the cwd for the `jj git clone` subprocess.
183	///
184	/// `jj git clone --colocate` does git clone + jj init + automatic
185	/// bookmark tracking in one step (unlike colocate-after-bare-git-clone,
186	/// which needs explicit `jj bookmark track`). The `verify_tracking`
187	/// call asserts the post-clone state matches expectations.
188	pub(crate) fn clone_one(
189	    source: &str,
190	    target_dir: &Path,
191	    parent_dir: &Path,
192	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
193	    let target_str = target_dir
194	        .to_str()
195	        .ok_or("target path is not valid UTF-8")?;
196	    info!("Cloning {source} → {target_str}...");
197	    run(
198	        "jj",
199	        &["git", "clone", "--colocate", source, target_str],
200	        parent_dir,
201	    )?;
202	    crate::common::verify_tracking(target_dir, "main")?;
203	    Ok(())
204	}
205	
206	/// Orchestrate a dual-repo clone: code via `clone_one`, bot via
207	/// `clone_one` (no graceful skip — `--scope=code,bot` requires
208	/// both sides; users who want code-only should use `--scope=por`),
209	/// then create the Claude Code symlink.
210	pub(crate) fn clone_dual(
211	    code_source: &str,
212	    target_dir: &Path,
213	    parent_dir: &Path,
214	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
215	    let session_source = derive_session_url(code_source);
216	    let session_dir = target_dir.join(".claude");
217	
218	    clone_one(code_source, target_dir, parent_dir)?;
219	    clone_one(&session_source, &session_dir, target_dir)?;
220	    info!("Creating Claude Code symlink...");
221	    let sl = symlink::install(target_dir)?;
222	
223	    info!("");
224	    info!("Done! Project cloned to {}", target_dir.display());
225	    info!("  Code repo:    {}", target_dir.display());
226	    info!("  Session repo: {}", session_dir.display());
227	    info!(
228	        "  Symlink:      {} -> {}",
229	        sl.symlink_path.display(),
230	        sl.abs_target.display()
231	    );
232	
233	    Ok(())
234	}
```

> AGENT

Clone tests pass (7 tests). Unit B complete.

**Unit B summary** (clone.rs):
- Module docs: replaced `--scope=code,bot` / `--scope=por` with default vs `--por` framing.
- Imports: `ScopeKind, parse_scope_kind` → `PorFlag`.
- `CloneArgs`: `pub scope: ScopeKind` → `pub por: PorFlag` (flattened leaf).
- `CloneParams`: `pub scope: ScopeKind` → `pub por: bool`.
- `From<&CloneArgs>`: `scope: a.scope` → `por: a.por.value`.
- Body: `match params.scope { Por / CodeBot }` → `if params.por { … } else { … }` (both the dry-run dispatch and the real dispatch).
- `clone_dual` doc: dropped the `--scope=code,bot` / `--scope=por` references.
- Tests: pruned 5 obsolete `scope_*` tests; updated `defaults` and `all_opts`; added a `por_flag` smoke test.

Bonus: refined `PorFlag` to use the `value`-field convention from options_flags/README (`#[arg(long = "por")] pub value: bool`) so consumers read `args.por.value` rather than `args.por.por`.

Stopping for review. Next is Unit C — migrate `init/params.rs` + `init.rs`.

```
1	//! Flat plain-struct input to the init op.
2	//!
3	//! `InitParams` is the clap-free shape that `pub fn init` reads
4	//! from. Built `From<&InitArgs>` at the binary edge (main.rs);
5	//! callers without clap (tests, future TUI / library embedding)
6	//! construct it directly.
7	//!
8	//! Per `notes/chores/chores-09.md > ## InitParams implementation
9	//! (0.44.0)`: domain types pass through (`RepoSelector`,
10	//! `ScopeKind`, `ConfigKind`, `PushRetryOptions`); leaf clap
11	//! wrappers do not.

13	use crate::config::RepoSelector;
14	use crate::init::InitArgs;
15	use crate::options_flags::config::ConfigKind;

19	/// Inputs to the init op, flat, owned, clap-free.
20	///
21	/// - `target`: the TARGET positional argument (URL, owner/name,
22	///   path, or bare NAME).
23	/// - `name`: optional `NAME` positional override.
24	/// - `account`: `--account` value (None ⇒ default account chain).
25	/// - `repo`: `--repo` value parsed into a `RepoSelector`.
26	/// - `scope`: `--scope` resolved (default `code,bot`).
27	/// - `private`: `--private`.
28	/// - `dry_run`: `--dry-run`.
29	/// - `push_retry`: `--push-retries` / `--push-retry-delay`.
30	/// - `use_template`: `--use-template` value.
31	/// - `config`: resolved `--config` value (None ⇒ canned write).
32	/// - `create_symlink`: whether `init` should create the
33	///   `~/.claude/projects/` symlink for dual-scope runs (production
34	///   path = true; test fixtures suppress with false).
35	pub struct InitParams {
36	    pub target: String,
37	    pub name: Option<String>,
38	    pub account: Option<String>,
39	    pub repo: Option<RepoSelector>,
40	    pub scope: ScopeKind,
41	    pub private: bool,
42	    pub dry_run: bool,
43	    pub push_retry: PushRetryOptions,
44	    pub use_template: Option<String>,
45	    pub config: Option<ConfigKind>,
46	    pub create_symlink: bool,
47	}
48	
49	impl From<&InitArgs> for InitParams {
50	    /// Convert clap-derived `InitArgs` into the flat `InitParams`.
51	    ///
52	    /// Production path: sets `create_symlink: true`. Tests that
53	    /// need `create_symlink: false` construct `InitParams`
54	    /// directly (or post-edit the field).
55	    fn from(a: &InitArgs) -> Self {
56	        Self {
57	            target: a.target.clone(),
58	            name: a.name.clone(),
59	            account: a.account.account.clone(),
60	            repo: a.repo.repo.clone(),
61	            scope: a.scope.scope,
62	            private: a.provision.private.private,
63	            dry_run: a.provision.dry_run.dry_run,
64	            push_retry: a.provision.push_retry.clone(),
65	            use_template: a.use_template.use_template.clone(),
66	            config: a.config.resolve(ConfigKind::None),
67	            create_symlink: true,
68	        }
69	    }
70	}
71
```

```
84	/// Run a command with retries, sleeping between attempts.
85	fn run_retry(
86	    cmd: &str,
87	    args: &[&str],
88	    cwd: &Path,
89	    retry: &PushRetryOptions,
90	) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
91	    let mut last_err = String::new();
92	    for attempt in 1..=retry.push_retries {
93	        match run(cmd, args, cwd) {
94	            Ok(out) => {
95	                if attempt > 1 {
96	                    debug!("succeeded after {attempt} attempts");
97	                }
98	                return Ok(out);
99	            }
100	            Err(e) => {
101	                last_err = e.to_string();
102	                if attempt < retry.push_retries {
103	                    debug!(
104	                        "attempt {attempt}/{} failed: {last_err}",
105	                        retry.push_retries
106	                    );
107	                    debug!("retrying in {}s...", retry.push_retry_delay);
108	                    std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs(retry.push_retry_delay));
109	                }
110	            }
111	        }
112	    }
113	    Err(format!("failed after {} attempts: {last_err}", retry.push_retries).into())
114	}
```

```
17	#[test]
18	fn defaults() {
19	    let args = parse(&["vc-x1", "init", "owner/repo"]);
20	    assert_eq!(args.target, "owner/repo");
21	    assert!(args.name.is_none());
22	    assert!(args.account.account.is_none());
23	    assert!(args.repo.repo.is_none());
24	    assert_eq!(args.scope.scope, ScopeKind::CodeBot);
25	    assert!(!args.provision.private.private);
26	    assert!(!args.provision.dry_run.dry_run);

28	    assert_eq!(args.provision.push_retry.push_retry_delay, 3);
29	    assert!(args.use_template.use_template.is_none());
30	}
```

```
400	// Unit tests for derive_session_url / derive_name live in
401	// src/repo_url.rs alongside the lifted functions.
402	
403	#[test]
404	fn expand_vars_tilde() {
405	    let prev = std::env::var("HOME").ok();
406	    // SAFETY: env-var manipulation is genuinely racy with parallel
407	    // test runners. Tests touching $VAR and $HOME live in this
408	    // module; if flakiness emerges, run with --test-threads=1.
409	    unsafe {
410	        std::env::set_var("HOME", "/h");
411	    }
412	    assert_eq!(expand_vars("~/foo").unwrap(), "/h/foo");
413	    assert_eq!(expand_vars("~").unwrap(), "/h");
414	    // SAFETY: see above.
415	    unsafe {
416	        match prev {
417	            Some(v) => std::env::set_var("HOME", v),
418	            None => std::env::remove_var("HOME"),
419	        }
420	    }
421	}
422	
423	#[test]
424	fn expand_vars_envvar() {
425	    let key = "VC_X1_TEST_EXPAND_VAR";
426	    // SAFETY: see expand_vars_tilde test.
427	    unsafe {
428	        std::env::set_var(key, "VAL");
429	    }
430	    assert_eq!(expand_vars("$VC_X1_TEST_EXPAND_VAR/x").unwrap(), "VAL/x");
431	    assert_eq!(expand_vars("a${VC_X1_TEST_EXPAND_VAR}b").unwrap(), "aVALb");
432	    assert_eq!(expand_vars("$/no-name").unwrap(), "$/no-name");
433	    assert!(expand_vars("$VC_X1_TEST_DEFINITELY_UNSET_xyz").is_err());
434	    // SAFETY: see expand_vars_tilde test.
435	    unsafe {
436	        std::env::remove_var(key);
437	    }
438	}
439	
440	#[test]
441	fn expand_vars_unterminated_brace_errors() {
442	    assert!(expand_vars("${UNCLOSED").is_err());
443	}
```

```
140	impl Drop for Fixture {
141	    /// Remove the fixture tree on drop. Best-effort; a failure here

153	/// Owned single-repo (POR) fixture with RAII cleanup.
154	///
155	/// Sibling of `Fixture` for `--scope=por` flows. Drives
156	/// `init::init` with `ScopeKind::Por` and a path
157	/// TARGET; `--repo local=<base>` steers the bare origin to
158	/// `<base>/remote.git` (vs. dual's `remote-code.git` /
159	/// `remote-claude.git`). No `.claude/` peer, no symlink.
160	///
161	/// Field shape differs from `Fixture` — there is no `claude` peer
162	/// path — so it's a distinct type rather than an `Option<PathBuf>`
163	/// on `Fixture` (the latter would force every dual-using caller to
164	/// `.unwrap()` or pattern-match).
165	pub struct FixturePor {
166	    /// Root tempdir that owns the repo and its bare-git remote.
167	    pub base: PathBuf,
168	    /// POR repo path (`<base>/work`).
169	    pub work: PathBuf,
170	}
171	
172	impl FixturePor {
173	    /// Build a fresh POR fixture in a unique tempdir.
174	    pub fn new(tag: &str) -> Self {
175	        Self::new_with_config(tag, None)
176	    }
177	
178	    /// Build a fresh POR fixture, threading `config` through to
179	    /// `InitArgs` for `--config` variant testing.
180	    pub fn new_with_config(tag: &str, config: Option<String>) -> Self {
181	        let base = unique_base(tag);
182	        // Path TARGET = `<base>/work`; basename ("work") becomes
183	        // the repo name. `--repo local=<base>` sets the bare-repo
184	        // parent, producing the layout:
185	        //   <base>/work/        (POR repo)
186	        //   <base>/remote.git   (bare origin)
187	        let work_path = base.join("work");
188	        let args = InitArgs {
189	            target: work_path.to_string_lossy().into_owned(),
190	            name: None,
191	            account: AccountOption::default(),
192	            repo: RepoOption {
193	                repo: Some(RepoSelector {
194	                    category: "local".to_string(),
195	                    value: Some(base.to_string_lossy().into_owned()),
196	                }),
197	            },
198	            scope: ScopeOption {
199	                scope: ScopeKind::Por,
200	            },
201	            provision: ProvisionOptionFlagBundle::default(),
202	            use_template: UseTemplateOption::default(),
203	            config: ConfigOption { raw: config },
204	        };
205	        let ctx = Context::load(None).expect("load user config for POR test fixture");
206	        let mut params = InitParams::from(&args);
207	        params.create_symlink = false;
208	        init(&ctx, &params).expect("build test fixture via init (POR)");
209	
210	        let work = base.join("work");
211	        FixturePor { base, work }
212	    }
213	}
```

```json
  "new_string": "- Abandon: revert the cycle to a no-op with an\n  `### Outcome` note capturing what didn't fit.\n\n## refactor: add --por flag; delete options_flags/scope.rs (0.54.0-1)\n\nFirst substep. The creation-side topology question — \"dual\nworkspace or single repo?\" — was previously a value of\n`--scope` (`code,bot|por`) parsed into a `ScopeKind` enum.\nIt's more naturally a boolean: `--por` or not. The substep\nintroduces a `PorFlag` boolean leaf in `options_flags/por.rs`\nand migrates `init` / `clone` / `test_helpers` to consume it;\nthe old `options_flags/scope.rs` (`ScopeKind`, `ScopeOption`,\n`parse_scope_kind`, `ScopeParser`) is deleted.\n\n- `PorFlag` is the first leaf written to the `value`-field\n  convention from\n  [options_flags/README.md](README.md#adding-a-new-leaf):\n  the inner field is `value`, the flag is declared via\n  `#[arg(long = \"por\")]`, and consumers read `args.por.value`\n  rather than the doubled `args.por.por`. Existing\n  single-field leaves (`dry_run`, `private`, `account`,\n  `config`, `use_template`, `repo`) predate this and are\n  tracked for migration in `notes/todo.md > #2`.\n- Consumer dispatch collapses from a two-arm `match scope\n  { CodeBot / Por }` to a single boolean check. Both the\n  dry-run print path and the real dispatch path in `init`\n  / `clone` shrink; the error messages follow the same\n  rename (`--scope=por` → `--por`,\n  `--config is only valid with --scope=por` →\n  `--config is only valid with --por`).\n- Tests prune the obsolete `--scope=*` / `-s *` parses —\n  no value space left for `--scope` to error on. Net unit\n  test count drops 10: 5 clone `scope_*` tests + 1 init\n  `scope_short_flag_por` + 6 `options_flags::scope_kind_*`\n  tests deleted; 2 new `por_flag*` smoke tests added.\n  366 unit + 14 integration tests pass.\n- `Cargo.toml`: `0.54.0-0` → `0.54.0-1`.\n- `notes/chores/chores-11.md`: backfilled `Commits: [[8]]`\n  on the 0.54.0-0 opener.\n- `notes/todo.md`: 0.54.0-0 → `(done)`; 0.54.0-1 marked\n  `(current)` (start of substep), flipped to `(done)`\n  here (end of substep).\n\n# References"
}
```
